\pgfsys@invoke{ }\pgfsys@endscope{{{}}}{}{}\hss}\pgfsys@discardpath\pgfsys@invoke{ }\pgfsys@endscope\hss}}\endpgfpicture}}_{ijji;k}]\overset{\eqref{eq:SM-factorization}}{\simeq}-\sum_{k}\langle\!\langle(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{P}_{k})^{2}\hat{\Delta}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle\ {\mathcal{G}}_{ij}+(i\leftrightarrow j)+{\cal O}(N^{-4+\alpha/2}),(S74)

where we recognize the second additional contribution in Eq. (17) of the main text,ai,t(2):=N2​∑k=0N−1⟨⟨(∂n^iP^k)2​Δ^k⟩⟩t.a^{(2)}_{i,t}:=N^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\langle\!\langle(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{P}_{k})^{2}\hat{\Delta}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle_{t}.(S75)

Interestingly, the meso-hydro contact terms linear in∂n^P^\partial_{\hat{n}}\hat{P}coming from thebanddcontributions cancel to leading order in1/N1/N, leaving𝒮2qseep​(x,y){\cal S}_{2}^{\text{qseep}}(x,y)as the only contact source of the equation.


From Eqs. (4) and (S74), and using the definition in Eq. (19) of the main text, one recovers Eq. (18) of the main text for the evolution of the two-replica coherence loop.

## 1Microscopic expression of the mass term

In this section, we compute explicitly the mass term in Eq. (19) of the main text in terms of the microscopic parameters entering the interaction dressing (1)-(3) of the main text. We separate the two contributions introduced in Eq. (17).


Recall,P^i=D0​(1+λ​p^i),withp^i=∑r≥1∑Q⊂SN|Q|=rbr|SN|r​∏q∈Qn^i+q.\hat{P}_{i}=\sqrt{D_{0}}\big(1+\lambda\hat{p}_{i}\big),\quad\text{with}\quad\hat{p}_{i}=\sum_{r\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\subset S_{N}\\
|Q|=r\end{subarray}}\frac{b_{r}}{|S_{N}|^{r}}\prod_{q\in Q}\hat{n}_{i+q}.

From these definitions, one obtains for the first termm(1)2​(x;τ):=limN→∞D0−1​ai,t(1)|x=i/Nτ=t/N2=2​limN→∞N2​λ2​κ​(p^i,p^i)|i=x​Nt=τ​N2.\displaystyle m^{2}_{(1)}(x;\tau):=\lim_{N\to\infty}D_{0}^{-1}a^{(1)}_{i,t}\Big|_{\begin{subarray}{c}x=i/N\\
\tau=t/N^{2}\end{subarray}}=2\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{2}\lambda^{2}\kappa(\hat{p}_{i},\hat{p}_{i})\Big|_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=xN\\
t=\tau N^{2}\end{subarray}}.(S76)

The cumulant readsκ​(p^i,p^i)\displaystyle\kappa(\hat{p}_{i},\hat{p}_{i})=∑r,s≥1∑Q,K⊂SN|Q|=r,|K|=sbr​bs|SN|r+s​κ​(∏q∈Qn^i+q,∏k∈Kn^i+k).\displaystyle=\sum_{r,s\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q,K\subset S_{N}\\
|Q|=r,\ |K|=s\end{subarray}}\frac{b_{r}b_{s}}{|S_{N}|^{r+s}}\ \kappa\big(\prod_{q\in Q}\hat{n}_{i+q},\prod_{k\in K}\hat{n}_{i+k}\big).(S77)

Using the MFT factorization (S15), and to leading order in1/N1/N, the cumulant becomesκ​(∏q∈Qn^i+q,∏k∈Kn^i+k)=⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩|Q∪K|−⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩|Q|+|K|+𝒪​(|SN|/N).\kappa\big(\prod_{q\in Q}\hat{n}_{i+q},\prod_{k\in K}\hat{n}_{i+k}\big)=\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle^{|Q\cup K|}-\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle^{|Q|+|K|}+{\cal O}(|S_{N}|/N).(S78)

Therefore,κ​(p^i,p^i)\displaystyle\kappa(\hat{p}_{i},\hat{p}_{i})=∑r,s≥1∑Q,K⊂SN|Q|=r,|K|=sbr​bs|SN|r+s​(⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩|Q∪K|−⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩r+s)+𝒪​(|SN|/N).\displaystyle=\sum_{r,s\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q,K\subset S_{N}\\
|Q|=r,\ |K|=s\end{subarray}}\frac{b_{r}b_{s}}{|S_{N}|^{r+s}}\left(\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle^{|Q\cup K|}-\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle^{r+s}\right)+{\cal O}(|S_{N}|/N).(S79)

We now group the pairsQ,K⊂SNQ,K\subset S_{N}according to their overlapℓ:=|Q∩K|\ell:=|Q\cap K|. For fixed(r,s,ℓ)(r,s,\ell), one has|Q∪K|=r+s−ℓ|Q\cup K|=r+s-\ell, and therefore⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩|Q∪K|−⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩r+s=⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩r+s−ℓ​(1−⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩ℓ)=12​σ0​[⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩]​∑p=0ℓ−1⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩r+s−ℓ−1+p.\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle^{|Q\cup K|}-\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle^{r+s}=\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle^{r+s-\ell}\left(1-\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle^{\ell}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{0}[\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle]\sum_{p=0}^{\ell-1}\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle^{r+s-\ell-1+p}.(S80)

Thus, a given overlap sector contributes to all powersm=r+s−ℓ−1,…,r+s−2m=r+s-\ell-1,\dots,r+s-2of the density. It follows that,κ​(p^i,p^i)\displaystyle\kappa(\hat{p}_{i},\hat{p}_{i})=12​|SN|​σ0​[⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩]​(∑m≥0Am(1)​⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩m)+𝒪​(|SN|/N),\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2|S_{N}|}\sigma_{0}[\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle]\Big(\sum_{m\geq 0}A^{(1)}_{m}\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle^{m}\Big)+{\cal O}(|S_{N}|/N),(S81)

withAm(1)=∑r,s≥1r+s≥m+2∑ℓ=max⁡(1,r+s−m−1)min⁡(r,s)br​bs|SN|r+s−1​𝒩r,s,ℓ.A^{(1)}_{m}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,s\geq 1\\
r+s\geq m+2\end{subarray}}\sum_{\ell=\max(1,r+s-m-1)}^{\min(r,s)}\frac{b_{r}b_{s}}{|S_{N}|^{r+s-1}}\,{\cal N}_{r,s,\ell}.(S82)

Here,𝒩r,s,ℓ=(|SN|ℓ)​(|SN|−ℓr−ℓ)​(|SN|−rs−ℓ){\cal N}_{r,s,\ell}=\binom{|S_{N}|}{\ell}\binom{|S_{N}|-\ell}{r-\ell}\binom{|S_{N}|-r}{s-\ell}(S83)

counts the number of pairs of subsetsQ,K⊂SNQ,K\subset S_{N}such that|Q|=r|Q|=r,|K|=s|K|=s, and|Q∩K|=ℓ|Q\cap K|=\ell. Binomial coefficients with inadmissible entries are understood to vanish. In the scaling limit,m(1)2​(x;τ)=cλ​σ0​[n¯​(x;τ)]​(∑m≥0Am(1)​n¯​(x;τ)m),cλ=λ2/λ∗2=𝒪​(1).m^{2}_{(1)}(x;\tau)=c_{\lambda}\,\sigma_{0}[\bar{n}(x;\tau)]\Big(\sum_{m\geq 0}A^{(1)}_{m}\,\bar{n}(x;\tau)^{m}\Big),\quad c_{\lambda}=\lambda^{2}/\lambda_{*}^{2}={\cal O}(1).(S84)

We now turn to the second contribution, defined asm(2)2​(x;τ):=limN→∞D0−1​ai,t(2)|x=i/Nτ=t/N2=limN→∞N2​λ2​∑k:{i−k}∈SN⟨⟨(∂n^ip^k)2​Δ^k⟩⟩|i=x​Nt=τ​N2.m^{2}_{(2)}(x;\tau):=\lim_{N\to\infty}D_{0}^{-1}a^{(2)}_{i,t}\Big|_{\begin{subarray}{c}x=i/N\\
\tau=t/N^{2}\end{subarray}}=\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{2}\lambda^{2}\sum_{k:\{i-k\}\in S_{N}}\langle\!\langle(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{k})^{2}\hat{\Delta}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle\Big|_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=xN\\
t=\tau N^{2}\end{subarray}}.(S85)

Using again (S15), one has⟨⟨(∂n^ip^k)2​Δ^k⟩⟩=⟨⟨(∂n^ip^k)2⟩⟩​⟨⟨Δ^k⟩⟩=⟨⟨(∂n^ip^k)2⟩⟩​σ0​[⟨⟨n^k⟩⟩]​(1+𝒪​(1/N)).\langle\!\langle(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{k})^{2}\hat{\Delta}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle=\langle\!\langle(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{k})^{2}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\!\langle\hat{\Delta}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle=\langle\!\langle(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{k})^{2}\rangle\!\rangle\sigma_{0}[\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle]\left(1+{\cal O}(1/N)\right).(S86)

Moreover,∂n^ip^k=∑r≥1∑R∪{i−k}⊂SN|R|=r−1br|SN|r​∏q∈Rn^k+q.\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{k}=\sum_{r\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\cup\{i-k\}\subset S_{N}\\
|R|=r-1\end{subarray}}\frac{b_{r}}{|S_{N}|^{r}}\prod_{q\in R}\hat{n}_{k+q}.(S87)

Therefore,⟨⟨(∂n^ip^k)2⟩⟩\displaystyle\langle\!\langle(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{k})^{2}\rangle\!\rangle=∑r,s≥1br​bs|SN|r+s​∑R,L⊂SN∖{i−k}|R|=r−1,|L|=s−1⟨⟨∏q∈R∪Ln^k+q⟩⟩=∑r,s≥1br​bs|SN|r+s​∑R,L⊂SN∖{i−k}|R|=r−1,|L|=s−1⟨⟨n^k⟩⟩|R∪L|+𝒪​(|SN|/N).\displaystyle=\sum_{r,s\geq 1}\frac{b_{r}b_{s}}{|S_{N}|^{r+s}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R,L\subset S_{N}\setminus\{i-k\}\\
|R|=r-1,\ |L|=s-1\end{subarray}}\langle\!\langle\prod_{q\in R\cup L}\hat{n}_{k+q}\rangle\!\rangle=\sum_{r,s\geq 1}\frac{b_{r}b_{s}}{|S_{N}|^{r+s}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R,L\subset S_{N}\setminus\{i-k\}\\
|R|=r-1,\ |L|=s-1\end{subarray}}\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle^{|R\cup L|}+{\cal O}(|S_{N}|/N).(S88)

The number of pairs(R,L)(R,L)with|R∪L|=m|R\cup L|=mis∑R,L⊂SN∖{a}|R|=r−1,|L|=s−1|R∪L|=m1=(|SN|−1m)​(mr−1)​(r−1r+s−2−m).\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R,L\subset S_{N}\setminus\{a\}\\
|R|=r-1,\ |L|=s-1\\
|R\cup L|=m\end{subarray}}1=\binom{|S_{N}|-1}{m}\binom{m}{r-1}\binom{r-1}{r+s-2-m}.(S89)

Thus, definingAm(2):=∑r,s≥1br​bs|SN|r+s−2​(|SN|−1m)​(mr−1)​(r−1r+s−2−m),A^{(2)}_{m}:=\sum_{r,s\geq 1}\frac{b_{r}b_{s}}{|S_{N}|^{r+s-2}}\binom{|S_{N}|-1}{m}\binom{m}{r-1}\binom{r-1}{r+s-2-m},(S90)

one obtains⟨⟨(∂n^ip^k)2⟩⟩=1|SN|2​(∑m≥0Am(2)​⟨⟨n^k⟩⟩m).\langle\!\langle(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{k})^{2}\rangle\!\rangle=\frac{1}{|S_{N}|^{2}}\Big(\sum_{m\geq 0}A^{(2)}_{m}\,\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle^{m}\Big).(S91)

Finally, summing over the|SN||S_{N}|values ofkksuch that{i−k}∈SN\{i-k\}\in S_{N}, and taking the large-scale limit, givesm(2)2​(x;τ)=cλ​σ0​[n¯​(x;τ)]​(∑m≥0Am(2)​n¯​(x;τ)m),cλ=λ2/λ∗2.m^{2}_{(2)}(x;\tau)=c_{\lambda}\,\sigma_{0}[\bar{n}(x;\tau)]\Big(\sum_{m\geq 0}A^{(2)}_{m}\,\bar{n}(x;\tau)^{m}\Big),\quad c_{\lambda}=\lambda^{2}/\lambda_{*}^{2}.(S92)

Both contributions are finite in the scaling regimeλ∼λ∗\lambda\sim\lambda_{*}. The full mass is then obtained asm2​(x;τ)=m(1)2​(x;τ)+m(2)2​(x;τ)=\displaystyle m^{2}(x;\tau)=m^{2}_{(1)}(x;\tau)+m^{2}_{(2)}(x;\tau)=σ0​[n¯​(x;τ)]​∑m≥0cλ​(Am(1)+Am(2))​n¯​(x;τ)m\displaystyle\sigma_{0}[\bar{n}(x;\tau)]\sum_{m\geq 0}c_{\lambda}\big(A_{m}^{(1)}+A_{m}^{(2)}\big)\bar{n}(x;\tau)^{m}=\displaystyle=∑m≥1Am​n¯​(x;τ)m,with​Am:=2​cλ​[Am−1(1)+Am−1(2)−Am−2(1)−Am−2(2)],\displaystyle\sum_{m\geq 1}A_{m}\bar{n}(x;\tau)^{m},\quad\text{with}\;A_{m}:=2c_{\lambda}\left[A^{(1)}_{m-1}+A^{(2)}_{m-1}-A^{(1)}_{m-2}-A^{(2)}_{m-2}\right],(S93)

where, by convention,A−1(1)=A−1(2)=0A^{(1)}_{-1}=A^{(2)}_{-1}=0.


For the short-range example in Eq. (4) of the main text, usingb1=2b_{1}=2,b2=−8b_{2}=-8and|SN|=2|S_{N}|=2, one obtains the coefficientsA0=0,A1=2​cλ​b12=8​cλ,A2=cλ​(−2​b12+2​b1​b2+38​b22)=−16​cλ,\displaystyle A_{0}=0,\qquad A_{1}=2c_{\lambda}b_{1}^{2}=8c_{\lambda},\qquad A_{2}=c_{\lambda}\left(-2b_{1}^{2}+2b_{1}b_{2}+\frac{3}{8}b_{2}^{2}\right)=-16c_{\lambda},A3=cλ​(−2​b1​b2−14​b22)=16​cλ,A4=−18​cλ​b22=−8​cλ,\displaystyle A_{3}=c_{\lambda}\left(-2b_{1}b_{2}-\frac{1}{4}b_{2}^{2}\right)=16c_{\lambda},\qquad A_{4}=-\frac{1}{8}c_{\lambda}b_{2}^{2}=-8c_{\lambda},(S94)

andAm>4=0A_{m>4}=0. This gives,m2​[n¯]=A1​n¯+A2​n¯2+A3​n¯3+A4​n¯4=2​cλ​σ0​(n¯)​[2−σ0​(n¯)].m^{2}[\bar{n}]=A_{1}\bar{n}+A_{2}\bar{n}^{2}+A_{3}\bar{n}^{3}+A_{4}\bar{n}^{4}=2c_{\lambda}\sigma_{0}(\bar{n})\big[2-\sigma_{0}(\bar{n})\big].(S95)

Equivalently, this result can be obtained from a direct calculation. Using Eq. (4), one hasp^i2=p^i\hat{p}_{i}^{2}=\hat{p}_{i}and thereforeD0−1​ai,t(1)=2​N2​λ2​(⟨⟨p^i2⟩⟩t−⟨⟨p^i⟩⟩t2)=2​cλ​σ0​[⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩t]​(1−σ0​[⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩t]).D_{0}^{-1}a^{(1)}_{i,t}=2N^{2}\lambda^{2}\left(\langle\!\langle\hat{p}_{i}^{2}\rangle\!\rangle_{t}-\langle\!\langle\hat{p}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle_{t}^{2}\right)=2c_{\lambda}\,\sigma_{0}[\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle_{t}]\big(1-\sigma_{0}[\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle_{t}]\big).(S96)

For the second contribution in Eq. (17) of the main text,∂n^ip^i+1=1−2​n^i+3,∂n^ip^i−2=1−2​n^i−3,\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{i+1}=1-2\hat{n}_{i+3},\qquad\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{i-2}=1-2\hat{n}_{i-3},(S97)

so that(∂n^ip^i+1)2=(∂n^ip^i−2)2=1(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{i+1})^{2}=(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{i-2})^{2}=1. Therefore,D0−1​ai,t(2)=N2​λ2​∑k:{i−k}∈SN⟨⟨(∂n^ip^k)2​Δ^k⟩⟩t=2​cλ​σ0​[⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩t].D_{0}^{-1}a^{(2)}_{i,t}=N^{2}\lambda^{2}\sum_{k:\{i-k\}\in S_{N}}\langle\!\langle(\partial_{\hat{n}_{i}}\hat{p}_{k})^{2}\hat{\Delta}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle_{t}=2c_{\lambda}\,\sigma_{0}[\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle_{t}].(S98)

Combining the two contributions, one recoversD0−1​(ai,t(1)+ai,t(2))=2​cλ​σ0​[⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩t]​(2−σ0​[⟨⟨n^i⟩⟩t]).D_{0}^{-1}\big(a^{(1)}_{i,t}+a^{(2)}_{i,t}\big)=2c_{\lambda}\sigma_{0}[\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle_{t}]\big(2-\sigma_{0}[\langle\!\langle\hat{n}_{i}\rangle\!\rangle_{t}]\big).(S99)

## 2Discussion of the case withn>2n>2replicas

In this section, we discuss the generalization of the two-replica result to arbitrary coherence loops. For thenn-replica loop, we label the legs cyclically,r=1,…,nr=1,\dots,n, with therrth leg corresponding toir→ir+1i_{r}\to i_{r+1}andin+1≡i1i_{n+1}\equiv i_{1}. The Itō contractions involve pairs of replicas(r,q)(r,q), with1≤r<q≤n1\leq r<q\leq n. The total number of such pairings is(n2)=n​(n−1)/2\binom{n}{2}=n(n-1)/2.


We distinguish the adjacent pairings along the loop,𝒜n:={(1,2),(2,3),…,(n−1,n),(1,n)},|𝒜n|=n,{\cal A}_{n}:=\{(1,2),(2,3),\dots,(n-1,n),(1,n)\},\qquad|{\cal A}_{n}|=n,(S100)

from the non-adjacent pairings, forn≥3n\geq 3,𝒩n:={(r,q):1≤r<q≤n}∖𝒜n,|𝒩n|=(n2)−n=n​(n−3)2.{\cal N}_{n}:=\{(r,q):1\leq r<q\leq n\}\setminus{\cal A}_{n},\qquad|{\cal N}_{n}|=\binom{n}{2}-n=\frac{n(n-3)}{2}.(S101)

We also introduce the shorthandar;k:=air​ir+1;k,br;k:=bir​ir+1;k,\hbox to11.4pt{\vbox to11.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\quad\lower-5.7pt\hbox to0.0pt{\pgfsys@beginscope\pgfsys@invoke{ }\definecolor{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@rgb@stroke{0}{0}{0}\pgfsys@invoke{ }\pgfsys@color@rgb@fill{0}{0}{0}\pgfsys@invoke{ }\pgfsys@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\pgfsys@invoke{ }\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\pgfsys@beginscope\pgfsys@invoke{ }{{{}}\hbox{\hbox{{\pgfsys@beginscope\pgfsys@invoke{ }\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0.15,0.15,.8}\pgfsys@color@rgb@stroke{0.15}{0.15}{.8}\pgfsys@invoke{ }{{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{\pgfsys@beginscope\pgfsys@invoke{ }\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0.15,0.15,.8}\pgfsys@color@rgb@stroke{0.15}{0.15}{.8}\pgfsys@invoke{ }{}\pgfsys@moveto{5.5pt}{0.0pt}\pgfsys@curveto{5.5pt}{3.0376pt}{3.0376pt}{5.5pt}{0.0pt}{5.5pt}\pgfsys@curveto{-3.0376pt}{5.5pt}{-5.5pt}{3.0376pt}{-5.5pt}{0.0pt}\pgfsys@curveto{-5.5pt}{-3.0376pt}{-3.0376pt}{-5.5pt}{0.0pt}{-5.5pt}\pgfsys@curveto{3.0376pt}{-5.5pt}{5.5pt}{-3.0376pt}{5.5pt}{0.0pt}\pgfsys@closepath\pgfsys@moveto{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\pgfsys@stroke\pgfsys@invoke{ }

Away from contact reductions they are subleading by the power counting discussed above.

We do not report the full calculation, since the intermediate expressions are lengthy and do not introduce new structures beyond Sec.3, the two-replica calculation, and Ref.[26]. As a final result, one obtains the hydrodynamic equation for the scaling functiongn​(x1,…,xn;τ):=limN→∞Nn−1​𝔼​[⟨X^i1,i2⟩t​…​⟨X^in,i1⟩t]c|ik=xk​Nt=τ​N2,g_{n}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n};\tau):=\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{n-1}{\mathbb{E}}[\langle\hat{X}_{i_{1},i_{2}}\rangle_{t}\dots\langle\hat{X}_{i_{n},i_{1}}\rangle_{t}]^{c}\Big|_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{k}=x_{k}N\\
t=\tau N^{2}\end{subarray}},(S103)

that is(∂τ−D0​∑r=1n[∂xr2−m2​(xr;τ)])​gn​(x1,…,xn;τ)=𝒮nqssep​(x1,…,xn;τ).\Big(\partial_{\tau}-D_{0}\sum_{r=1}^{n}\big[\partial_{x_{r}}^{2}-m^{2}(x_{r};\tau)\big]\Big)g_{n}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n};\tau)={\cal S}_{n}^{\rm qssep}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n};\tau).(S104)

Here,m2​(xr;τ)m^{2}(x_{r};\tau)is the same mass as in Eq. (19) of the main text, acting on each point of the loop. The QSSEP contact source is𝒮nQSSEP​(x1,…,xn;τ):=2​D0​∑1≤i<j≤nδ​(xi−xj)\displaystyle{\cal S}_{n}^{\rm QSSEP}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n};\tau):=2D_{0}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\delta(x_{i}-x_{j})(∂xign−p(xi,xj+1,…,xn,x1,…,xi−1⏟n−p;τ))×\displaystyle\big(\partial_{x_{i}}g_{n-p}(\underbrace{x_{i},x_{j+1},\dots,x_{n},x_{1},\dots,x_{i-1}}_{n-p};\tau)\big)\times×(∂xjgp​(xj,xi+1,…,xj−1⏟p;τ)),\displaystyle\times\big(\partial_{x_{j}}g_{p}(\underbrace{x_{j},x_{i+1},\dots,x_{j-1}}_{p};\tau)\big),(S105)

whereg1​(x;τ)≡n¯​(x;τ)g_{1}(x;\tau)\equiv\bar{n}(x;\tau). The boundary conditions aregn​(x1,…,xn)={na,nb;forn=1andx=0,1;0,forn≥2and somexi∈{0,1}.g_{n}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=\begin{cases}n_{a},n_{b};\quad\text{for $n=1$ and $x=0,1$};\\[5.0pt]
0,\quad\text{for $n\geq 2$ and some $x_{i}\in\{0,1\}$}.\end{cases}(S106)
